From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.00, F40.10, F41.9, F32.9, F33.9, F06.0, F06.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is… I’ve been having this thing where someone will be talking right to me, and I’m looking at them, you know, nodding along, but then I realize I missed part of what they said. Like at the museum, when they’re talking about the next art walk—those can get a little busy, depending on who’s organizing it—and I’ll catch the beginning and the end, but the middle is just gone. Then I have to ask them to run it by me again. It’s embarrassing, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know that I’d say depressed, exactly. I still like the things I like, I think. Some days I’m just a bit… I don’t know, flatter maybe, but then again that might just be normal at my age. My neighbor was saying the other day how winter light gets to him, and I started thinking about how the house feels different when the curtains are open—anyway, I’m not sure I’d call it losing interest. I haven’t really noticed that in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, you know? If I’ve been up moving around the house or running errands, sure. But I can’t say I’ve noticed some big change where I’m tired even after resting. Though sometimes I wonder if I’m just not paying attention to it—like my brother used to say he could tell by noon whether it was going to be “one of those days,” and I never knew what he meant by that. But no, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything definite.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more anxious, worried, or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Hmm… anxious? I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I worry about ordinary things, I suppose—bills, appointments, whether I locked the back door, that sort of thing. Actually, the back door sticks sometimes if the weather’s damp, which is annoying because you think it’s shut and then it isn’t quite—\n\nBut no, I haven’t really noticed myself feeling especially on edge lately. Not in a way that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or focusing on tasks, like reading or following a conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do notice that sometimes. Like, if I sit down with one of those gallery packets, or notes from something, I’ll start off fine. I’m reading it, thinking I’m taking it in, and then after a few minutes my eyes are still going along the page but my mind’s gone somewhere else entirely.\n\nThen I realize I don’t actually know what I just read, so I have to go back over the same paragraph again. It’s a bit annoying. I used to think maybe the print was too small—some of those packets are laid out terribly, you know—but it’s not always that. It’s more like I drift off without noticing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not most of the day, nearly every day. I mean, I have my off moments, like anybody. Sometimes I’ll sit around and feel sort of blah, especially if the weather’s gray—my wife used to say those winter afternoons make the whole house feel like it’s waiting for something.\n\nBut depressed, like all day for days on end? I haven’t really noticed that. I wouldn’t say that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take pleasure in things you usually enjoy, or where activities feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve really noticed that in a clear way. I mean, some days I don’t feel like starting things, but once I’m doing them it’s usually fine. Like I’ll put off sorting through a box of old photos, and then I find one from a trip years ago and suddenly I’m looking at all of them for an hour.\n\nSo… I wouldn’t say things I enjoy feel like a chore, exactly. At least not that I’ve paid much attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Hmm… I don’t really know. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower or talking slower, not that anyone’s pointed out to me anyway.\n\nRestless… I mean, I’ll shift around in a chair sometimes, but I think everybody does that, especially in those waiting room chairs—some of them feel like they were designed by someone who never sat down in their life. I remember at the DMV once, years ago, the chairs were so low I thought I’d need a crane to get back up.\n\nBut no, I don’t think I’d say I feel like I can’t sit still. Not in any big way that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your memory lately, like forgetting things more often than usual or struggling to remember information you would normally recall easily?\n\nPatient: Well… memory, I don’t know if it’s memory exactly. I can remember things from years ago better than where I put something ten minutes ago, which is kind of annoying.\n\nI’m always setting things down “somewhere safe,” you know—like the camera battery, or name tags, or an envelope with sign-up sheets—and then I spend half the morning going back and forth trying to figure out where I left it. I’ll think, “I put it right here so I wouldn’t lose it,” and then of course it’s not there.\n\nThat happened the other day with some papers, and I ended up checking the same two spots three times. So yes, I guess I have been misplacing things more than I’d like.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately, like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. My appetite seems about the same, I guess.\n\nWeight, I’m not sure—I don’t weigh myself all that often unless I’m at the doctor’s office. We used to have a scale in the bathroom, but it got shoved behind some towels or something, and then the battery died… those little round batteries, I never remember which size they are.\n\nBut no, I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, and I don’t think my clothes are fitting differently. So I’d say no big change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying about things more than usual, or feeling like you can’t control your worries?\n\nPatient: Worrying… hmm. I wouldn’t say more than usual, no. I mean, I worry about regular things—appointments, bills, whether I remembered to lock the door, that sort of thing.\n\nActually the lock thing is funny, because our old front door used to stick in the winter, and you had to kind of lift it while turning the key. Took me forever to get used to that.\n\nBut no, I don’t think I feel like my worries are out of control or anything like that. Nothing that’s really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt like nothing really mattered, or where you felt hopeless about things?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have days where I get irritated or sort of worn out, like anyone, but not like “nothing matters.”\n\nSometimes the news makes it feel like everything’s gone sideways, but then I have to stop watching it because they repeat the same three stories all morning anyway. My wife says I get sucked into the weather reports too, especially if they’re talking about snow.\n\nBut, no… I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling hopeless. Nothing like that recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you felt down or low most of the time for several days in a row, lasting for most of the past two years?\n\nPatient: For most of the past two years? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have low days here and there, sure, but not like several days in a row most of the time.\n\nTwo years is a long time to think back on… I’d have to look at a calendar, and even then, I get mixed up with when things happened. Like I remember one winter feeling kind of blah, but winter always does that a bit, you know? It gets dark early and then you’re just sitting there waiting for spring.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say I’ve been down or low most of the time for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or low more days than not over the past two years, even if they weren’t severe enough to interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say “more days than not,” honestly. I don’t really track it that way. Some days I’m a little low or just kind of flat, especially if the day gets away from me, but I don’t think it’s been most days for two years.\n\nTwo years is hard for me to picture—like, I remember things by seasons more than dates. Last fall we had that stretch where it rained constantly, and I kept meaning to clean out the gutters, which reminds me I still need to check the downspout by the garage…\n\nBut, sorry—no, I don’t think I’ve felt down more days than not. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t feel any enjoyment from things you usually like doing, where everything just feels flat or uninteresting?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. I still like things when I actually sit down with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn’t feel any enjoyment from things you usually like, or where activities felt completely uninteresting or flat?\n\nPatient: Recently? I don’t think so, not that I’ve really noticed. Nothing like that recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you felt down or low most of the time for several days in a row, lasting for most of the past two years?\n\nPatient: For most of the past two years? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have low days here and there, sure, but not like several days in a row most of the time.\n\nTwo years is a long time to think back on… I’d have to look at a calendar, and even then, I get mixed up with when things happened. Like I remember one winter feeling kind of blah, but winter always does that a bit, you know? It gets dark early and then you’re just sitting there waiting for spring.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say I’ve been down or low most of the time for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you felt down or low more days than not over the past two years, even if they weren’t severe enough to interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say “more days than not,” honestly. I don’t really track it that way. Some days I’m a little low or just kind of flat, especially if the day gets away from me, but I don’t think it’s been most days for two years.\n\nTwo years is hard for me to picture—like, I remember things by seasons more than dates. Last fall we had that stretch where it rained constantly, and I kept meaning to clean out the gutters, which reminds me I still need to check the downspout by the garage…\n\nBut, sorry—no, I don’t think I’ve felt down more days than not. At least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t feel any enjoyment from things you usually like doing, where everything just feels flat or uninteresting?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. I mean, sometimes I start something I usually like and then I wander off from it, but that’s not because it feels flat exactly.\n\nLike if I’m watching one of those old shows, I’ll still enjoy it, but then I’ll remember I was supposed to take the trash out, and then I see something in the garage that needs sorting, and the next thing I know the show’s been paused for half an hour.\n\nBut no, I wouldn’t say everything feels uninteresting. I still like things when I actually sit down with them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn’t feel any enjoyment from things you usually like, or where activities felt completely uninteresting or flat?\n\nPatient: Recently? I don’t think so, not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I’ll have times where I sit down to do something I like and then somehow I’m up again looking for something in the kitchen or checking a drawer for batteries—actually I need to buy batteries—but it’s not like the thing feels completely flat.\n\nIf I get myself to focus on it, I can still enjoy it. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where nothing felt enjoyable.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down or lost interest in things most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe enough to interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had a solid two-week stretch like that, no. Not where I was down or uninterested most of the time.\n\nI mean, I’ve had a few blah days here and there, but then something will catch my attention, or I’ll get into some little project—well, sometimes halfway into it and then I forget what I was doing. Like I started organizing a drawer last week and found an old flashlight, and that reminded me I should check the storm supplies…\n\nBut to answer you, no, I haven’t noticed a two-week period where I felt low or lost interest most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period lasting at least four days where your mood was unusually high, overly cheerful, or irritable, and you felt more energetic or restless than usual in a way that was noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Hmm… four days? I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything like that. I mean, I get restless sometimes, but not really in the way of being unusually cheerful or revved up where people are commenting on it.\n\nI can get irritable if something isn’t where I thought I put it—like the other day I was looking for a receipt and ended up going through a whole stack of mail, which I probably should’ve sorted months ago—but that’s just regular aggravation, I think.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think there’s been a stretch of several days where my mood was unusually high or anything noticeable like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get frustrated with myself sometimes, like when I can’t find something I just had in my hand, and then I’m suddenly in the closet looking for something else entirely—but hurting myself, or thinking life isn’t worth living? No, I haven’t had thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these times when you lose focus or can't remember where you put things—do they happen almost every day, and do they bother you enough that you've had to make changes in how you do things, like writing more notes or asking people to repeat themselves a lot?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that happens a lot—pretty close to every day, I’d say. The big one is putting something “somewhere safe,” which is always the kiss of death.\n\nLike I’ll have the camera battery, or the name tags, or an envelope with sign-up sheets, and I’ll set it down thinking, okay, this is a good spot. Then half the morning is me going back through the kitchen, the car, the little table by the door… and then I find something else I was supposed to deal with, so that pulls me off track too.\n\nIt’s not like a disaster, but it does bother me. I’ve had to kind of make piles or leave things right by the door, otherwise I lose them and spend way too much time retracing my steps."
